The two phases start in pressure equilibrium, with $T_\mathrm{cl} \approx 4 \times 10^4$ K; gas in photoionisation equilibrium with the ultraviolet background can be about twice cooler (footnote 1). Hot gas of density $\rho_\mathrm{wind}$ flows in at the upstream boundary, and all other boundaries let gas out. As in [McCourt et al.](#mccourt-et-al-2015) (2015), a cloud-tracking scheme changes the frame continuously to follow the cold gas (3.1). The boxes are nevertheless long, from 60 to $400\,r_\mathrm{cl}$ along the wind and 12 to $18\,r_\mathrm{cl}$ across it, to hold the tail (2.6.9). The runs use 8 cells per cloud radius, with tests at 32 and 64. The cooling follows a seven-piece power-law fit to the curve of [Sutherland and Dopita](#sutherland-dopita-1993) (1993), integrated after [Townsend](#townsend-2009) (2009), with a floor at $T_\mathrm{floor} = T_\mathrm{cl}$ that mimics heating. The variants lower the floor, use a 67-piece fit to the equilibrium curve of [Wiersma, Schaye and Smith](#wiersma-et-al-2009) (2009), add heating that returns the radiated energy uniformly, switch off cooling above $0.6\,T_\mathrm{wind}$, make the transverse boundaries periodic or raise $\mathcal{M}_\mathrm{wind}$ to 3 (§5.2). The radius changes with $\chi$ through $t_\mathrm{cool,cl}/t_\mathrm{cc}$, from $10^{-6}$ to $10^{-1}$. Cold gas is measured as gas denser than $\rho_\mathrm{cl}/3$, and its velocity is weighted by a dye (§4).

### 3.9 The cloud–wind runs of Abruzzo et al.

[Abruzzo, Bryan and Fielding](#abruzzo-bryan-fielding-2022) (2022, §3.1) use Enzo-E, a rewrite of Enzo ([Bryan et al.](#bryan-et-al-2014) 2014) for exascale computing on the adaptive framework Cello, here on a fixed uniform Cartesian grid in three dimensions. For this work they implemented the unsplit van Leer integrator with constrained transport (VL+CT) of [Stone and Gardiner](#stone-gardiner-2009) (2009), a second-order predictor–corrector scheme with second-order reconstruction, first-order in the predictor step, and the HLLD Riemann solver ([Miyoshi and Kusano](#miyoshi-kusano-2005) 2005), which reduces to HLLC without fields. A spherical cloud of radius $R_\mathrm{cl}$, at rest and in pressure equilibrium at $p/k_\mathrm{B} = 10^3\,\mathrm{cm^{-3}\,K}$, sits in a wind of $\mathcal{M}_\mathrm{w} = 1.5$ and density $\rho_\mathrm{cl}/\chi$. A passive scalar marks the gas of the cloud, and some runs add a uniform transverse field. The domain extends $100\,R_\mathrm{cl}$ downwind and $12\,R_\mathrm{cl}$ across. The wind enters $20\,R_\mathrm{cl}$ upwind of the cloud, and the other boundaries let gas out, which keeps $\nabla \cdot \mathbf{B} = 0$ about as precisely as periodic boundaries would (footnote 6). Waves and discontinuities reflected from the transverse boundaries raise the pressure after about $6\,t_\mathrm{cc}$, an artefact that grows with $\chi$ and that a three times wider domain only delays (§4.1).

Cooling comes from the library Grackle ([Smith et al.](#smith-et-al-2017) 2017) for solar metallicity, with the ultraviolet background of [Haardt and Madau](#haardt-madau-2012) (2012) and without self-shielding ([Abruzzo, Bryan and Fielding](#abruzzo-bryan-fielding-2022) 2022, §3.1). It is restricted to temperatures between about $T_\mathrm{cl}$ and $0.6\,T_\mathrm{w}$, by setting the rate to $10^{-99}$ in the tables elsewhere. By an oversight, Compton cooling off the cosmic microwave background remains there, with a cooling time near $10^4\,t_\mathrm{cc}$ (footnote 5). The frame follows the cloud (Appendix B). Every $0.0625\,t_\mathrm{cc}$, its velocity is set to the minimum velocity of the cells with a scalar density of at least $\rho_\mathrm{cl}/1000$, unless that would lower it, so that the bow shock stays in the domain. A bug froze the frame in all runs below 64 cells per radius, with negligible effect on the results (Fig. 21).

The letter of [Abruzzo, Fielding and Bryan](#abruzzo-fielding-bryan-2023) (2023, §2) uses the code and set-up of [Abruzzo, Fielding and Bryan](#abruzzo-fielding-bryan-2022) (2022), and takes its runs at $T_\mathrm{cl} \approx 5 \times 10^3$ K from there. It adds runs at $\chi = 100$ with $\mathcal{M}_\mathrm{w}$ of 3 and 6, and all runs at $4 \times 10^4$ K. All use 16 cells per cloud radius, the pre-tabulated solver of Grackle with the background of Haardt and Madau at redshift 0, a uniform solar metallicity and $p/k_\mathrm{B} = 10^3\,\mathrm{K\,cm^{-3}}$. Cooling is switched off below $T_\mathrm{cl}$, which hardly changes $t_\mathrm{cool}$ for clouds at $5 \times 10^3$ K (Fig. 1). It is also switched off above about $0.6\,T_\mathrm{w}$, except in the runs at $\chi = 10^4$ with $T_\mathrm{cl} \approx 5 \times 10^3$ K and at $\chi = 10^3$ with $4 \times 10^4$ K, whose winds cool slowly compared with $t_\mathrm{cc}$ (footnote 3).

### 3.10 The turbulent boxes of Gronke et al.

[Gronke et al.](#gronke-et-al-2022) (2022, §3) use Athena++ ([Stone et al.](#stone-et-al-2020) 2020) with the HLLC Riemann solver, second-order reconstruction with slope limiters in the primitive variables and the unsplit van Leer integrator ([Gardiner and Stone](#gardiner-stone-2008) 2008). Cooling follows the scheme of [Townsend](#townsend-2009) (2009) for a broken power-law fit to a curve of solar metallicity, with a floor at $T_\mathrm{floor} = 4 \times 10^4$ K. The floor matches earlier studies and keeps the gas fully ionised, as the rates assume collisional ionisation equilibrium (footnote 3), and $\ell_\mathrm{shatter}$ is evaluated there. $N_\mathrm{cl}$ droplets of size $r_\mathrm{d}$, overdensity $\chi_\mathrm{init}$ and temperature $T_\mathrm{cl,init}$, in pressure balance, are placed at random within a radius $d_0$, slightly potato-shaped against the carbuncle instability, with density perturbations of about 1 per cent throughout (footnote 4). An initial turbulent field is imposed, and the box is stirred continuously between wavenumbers $n_\mathrm{low}$ and $n_\mathrm{high}$, with a Kolmogorov spectrum, a correlation time $t_\mathrm{corr}$ and a solenoidal fraction $f_\mathrm{shear}$, to hold the turbulent Mach number roughly constant. The fiducial values are $n_\mathrm{low} = 0$, $n_\mathrm{high} = 2$, $d_0 = 15\,r_\mathrm{d}$, $T_\mathrm{cl,init} = 8 \times 10^4$ K, $\chi_\mathrm{init} = 50$, $f_\mathrm{shear} \sim 1/3$ and $t_\mathrm{corr} \sim 2t_\mathrm{eddy}$, in a periodic box of usually $40\,r_\mathrm{cl}$, which gives a cold mass fraction of about 0.003 for one cloud at $\chi \sim 100$. The clouds cool quickly to the floor, and the initial perturbation does not affect the evolution in well-stirred boxes. The cold gas is resolved by at least about 16 cells, with checks at about 4 and 64.

A subset with Lagrangian tracer particles uses FLASH ([Fryxell et al.](#fryxell-et-al-2000) 2000) with its unsplit Godunov solver and the same cooling ([Gronke et al.](#gronke-et-al-2022) 2022, §3). The box is first driven for about $2.4\,t_\mathrm{eddy}$, to a steady state of $\mathcal{M} \sim 0.4$, before a droplet is inserted by raising the density and lowering the temperature by $\chi_\mathrm{init}$ in a sphere of radius $r_\mathrm{cl}$. The driving matters little against the stochasticity of the runs (Appendix A). Driving only on small scales changes the morphology but not the growth, which supports $l \sim r_\mathrm{cl}$ in eq. 7, while purely solenoidal and uncorrelated driving slow the growth, in tests at 1/8 of the mass resolution.

### 3.11 The wind tunnels of Leary et al.

[Leary, Schneider and Richie](#leary-et-al-2024) (2024, §2) use Cholla ([Schneider and Robertson](#schneider-robertson-2015) 2015) with PPM reconstruction, the HLLC Riemann solver and the van Leer integrator, in a standard wind tunnel. A constant wind enters a box of $32 \times 16 \times 16\,R_\mathrm{cl}$ at its left boundary, and all other boundaries let gas out. The wind has $n_\mathrm{w} = 0.01\,\mathrm{cm^{-3}}$ and $T_\mathrm{w} = 10^6$ K and blows at 100 or $1000\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, given as Mach numbers of 0.852 and 8.52. The cloud, a sphere of radius 50 pc at rest with $\chi = 100$ and $T_\mathrm{cl} = 10^4$ K, starts $3.2\,R_\mathrm{cl}$ from the inflow boundary and drifts downstream through the box (Fig. 1). The runs last $10\,t_\mathrm{cc}$, without cooling. **[GK]** For a mean molecular weight of 0.6 and $\gamma = 5/3$, the sound speed of the wind is about $150\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, which gives Mach numbers of 0.66 and 6.6, the values of the later paper.

[Leary, Richie and Schneider](#leary-et-al-2026) (2026, §2) run four sets, adiabatic and radiative in each wind, with the same code, now with PPMP reconstruction ([Colella and Woodward](#colella-woodward-1984) 1984), the HLLC solver ([Toro, Spruce and Speares](#toro-et-al-1994) 1994; [Batten et al.](#batten-et-al-1997) 1997) and the van Leer integrator ([Stone and Gardiner](#stone-gardiner-2009) 2009). The radiative runs use a piecewise parabolic fit to an equilibrium cooling curve for solar metallicity, computed with Cloudy ([Ferland et al.](#ferland-et-al-2013) 2013) and cut off below $10^4$ K. Their boxes are extended to $48 \times 16 \times 16\,R_\mathrm{cl}$, since the clouds survive longer. The Mach numbers are 0.659 and 6.59, with $t_\mathrm{cc}$ of 4890 and 489 kyr. The runs last $10\,t_\mathrm{cc}$, the radiative supersonic one $20\,t_\mathrm{cc}$, and are measured every $0.2\,t_\mathrm{cc}$, or $0.4\,t_\mathrm{cc}$ for the latter. Thresholds for cloud material from a third of the initial density of the cloud down to three times that of the wind change the mass somewhat, but not the trends (Appendix A, Fig. 7).

### 3.12 The mixing layers of Lancaster et al.

[Lancaster et al.](#lancaster-et-al-2026) (2026, §3) solve the Euler equations with a source term for heating and cooling using AthenaK ([Stone et al.](#stone-et-al-2024) 2024), a code for graphics processors, with second-order piecewise linear reconstruction of the primitive variables, the HLLC Riemann solver ([Toro, Spruce and Speares](#toro-et-al-1994) 1994) and a second-order Runge–Kutta integrator. The box of $L_\mathrm{box} \times L_\mathrm{box} \times 1.5\,L_\mathrm{box}$, with $L_\mathrm{box} = \rho_0 = P_0 = 1$, is periodic in $x$ and $y$ and open at the bottom. At the top all conserved variables are held at the hot state except the vertical momentum, which has a zero gradient to allow inflow. A hot and a cold state of density contrast $\chi = T_\mathrm{hot}/T_\mathrm{cold}$, with $T = P/\rho$, meet at $z = 0$ through a tanh profile of thickness $\Delta x/2$ and shear at $v_\mathrm{rel}$. The source term, close to that of [Chen, Fielding and Bryan](#chen-et-al-2022) (2022), keeps both phases thermally stable and peaks in cooling at $T_\mathrm{pk} = (T_\mathrm{cold}^2 T_\mathrm{hot})^{1/3}$, with power laws on either side whose slopes scale with $1/\log_{10}\chi$ and a quadratic dependence on pressure (eqs. 11 to 16, Fig. 2). The parameters are $\xi = t_\mathrm{sh}/t_\mathrm{cool,min}$ from 1 to 1000, with $t_\mathrm{sh} = L_\mathrm{box}/v_\mathrm{rel}$, the Mach number $\mathcal{M} = v_\mathrm{rel}/c_\mathrm{s,hot}$ of 1/2 or 1/8, and $\chi$ of 30, 100 and 1000, with $\chi = 10$ in Appendix A, at 64 to 512 cells across the box (eqs. 18 to 21).

Every 10 steps a Galilean boost along $z$ removes the mean vertical velocity of the gas within a factor of $10^{0.1}$ of $T_\mathrm{pk}$, limited to $v_\mathrm{rel}/100$ and steered away from the boundaries ([Lancaster et al.](#lancaster-et-al-2026) 2026, §3.3, eq. 17). The runs last $30\,t_\mathrm{sh}$ and are analysed after $10\,t_\mathrm{sh}$, with 100 snapshots each. The area of the interface is measured on the fly by marching cubes on the isosurface $T = T_\mathrm{pk}$, and $v_\mathrm{diff}$ by the gradient across the layer of the velocity into it (§4, eq. 25). Only the component of the velocity structure function along $y$, across the shear and the inflow, traces the turbulence fairly, so $v_\mathrm{t}(\ell) = 3^{1/2}\,\mathrm{SF}_2(v_y; \ell)$ (eq. 28, Appendix B).

### 3.13 The fountain clouds of Marinacci et al.

[Marinacci et al.](#marinacci-et-al-2010) (2010, §3.1) use the Eulerian code ECHO ([Londrillo and Del Zanna](#londrillo-del-zanna-2004) 2004), which conserves fluxes and uses shock-capturing schemes of high order, on two-dimensional Cartesian grids of $384 \times 768$, $512 \times 1024$ (or $\times 1536$ for runs of 50 Myr) and $768 \times 1536$ cells. Ghost cells keep the pressure gradient at zero on the boundaries. Cooling follows [Sutherland and Dopita](#sutherland-dopita-1993) (1993), with the same metallicity in cloud and corona. The two dimensions model an infinite cylinder moving across its axis, and quantities per unit length convert to a sphere of the same radius through the length $\frac{4}{3}r$ over which the cylinder holds the mass of the sphere. The clouds weigh 0.44 to $4.4 \times 10^4\,M_\odot$, far below the Jeans mass of $1.6 \times 10^8\,M_\odot$, so self-gravity is neglected. Gas below $5 \times 10^5$ K counts as cool.

[Marinacci et al.](#marinacci-et-al-2011) (2011, §2) use the later version ECHO++ of the code ([Del Zanna and Bucciantini](#del-zanna-bucciantini-2002) 2002; [Del Zanna et al.](#del-zanna-et-al-2007) 2007), in which the metallicity is advected and the mean molecular weight depends on temperature, both entering the cooling after Sutherland and Dopita. The grid has $6144 \times 1024$ cells of about 2 pc, some 50 per cloud radius, with open boundaries on all sides (Table 1). The cloud masses of the table include the conversion from cylinder to sphere, and are higher than in the earlier paper at equal density because $\mu$ now varies with temperature. The runs at $10^{-3}\,\mathrm{cm^{-3}}$ cover $v_0$ of 75, 100, 150 and $200\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, each with and without cooling, and runs with cooling in coronae of $2 \times 10^{-3}$ and $5 \times 10^{-4}\,\mathrm{cm^{-3}}$ cover 50 to $100\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$. Coronal gas leaving through the open boundaries affects the momentum of the corona only in the last 10 Myr, so its measurement stops at 50 Myr (§3.3). A run at $100\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ on a domain twice as wide changed the coronal momentum by less than 8 per cent.
